# IAP vs. web checkout — research notes (compiled 17 Aug 2026)

**Flags:** `[IND]` independent · `[VENDOR]` seller of the thing being described · `[PRIMARY]` contract/statute/court/company filing · `[WEAK]` unsourced or SEO content, listed only to warn you off it.

---

## ⚠️ THREE THINGS THAT RESET THE PREMISE

**1. Apple filed a proposal four days ago to charge Small Business Program developers 5% on US link-outs.** Filed 13–14 Aug 2026 in N.D. Cal.: 15% standard, 10% for subscription renewals and partner programs, **5% for SBP**. ([TechCrunch, 14 Aug 2026](https://techcrunch.com/2026/08/14/apple-proposes-to-take-a-15-cut-of-purchases-made-outside-the-app-store/), [9to5Mac, 13 Aug 2026](https://9to5mac.com/2026/08/13/apple-proposes-commissions-of-up-to-15-for-off-app-store-purchases-in-the-us/)) `[IND]` Not in force yet — Judge Gonzalez Rogers decides. If accepted, "5% MoR vs 15% IAP" becomes roughly **10%+ vs 15%**.

**2. The "5% + $0.50 vs 15%" gap is much smaller than it looks, for two structural reasons.**
- **Apple's commission is charged on the tax-EXCLUSIVE price.** Schedule 2 §3.4(a), v126 (17 Dec 2025) `[PRIMARY]`: *"the prices payable by End-Users shall be net of any and all taxes collected."* Lemon Squeezy's fee is charged on the **tax-inclusive total** — confirmed by LS's own worked example ([docs.lemonsqueezy.com/help/getting-started/fees](https://docs.lemonsqueezy.com/help/getting-started/fees)): $20 product + $4 French VAT = $24 total, platform fee **$2.06** = $0.50 + 6.5% × $24.
- **Lemon Squeezy's real rate isn't 5%.** Same page, verbatim: **+1.5% international** (non-US), **+1.5% PayPal**, **+0.5% subscription payments**, **1% payout fee** for non-US bank accounts, **+5%** on cart-recovery-email payments.

My arithmetic (derived, not sourced): on a **$9.99/mo US subscription, domestic card**, LS = 5.5% + $0.50 ≈ **10.5%** vs Apple SBP **15%** — a 4.5pt gap, not 10. On a **€9.99/mo EU subscription, foreign card**, LS ≈ **13.4%** vs Apple **15.0%** — a **1.6pt gap**. On a **one-time $75 licence**, LS ≈ **8.5%** vs Apple **15%** — here the MoR wins decisively. **Ticket size and geography drive this more than the headline rates do.**

**3. Lemon Squeezy is converging into Stripe Managed Payments.** LS is live and onboarding at 5% + 50¢ today (pricing page fetched 17 Aug 2026, active signup CTA, no sunset notice) `[VENDOR-PRIMARY]`. But Stripe acquired it in Jul 2024 and LS is running a site-wide banner "2026 Update: Lemon Squeezy + Stripe Managed Payments." **The post body could not be read** (client-side rendered, 403). Stripe's own MoR is priced at **3.5% on top of Payments fees** = **~6.4% + $0.30 domestic, ~8–9% international** ([stripe.com/pricing](https://stripe.com/pricing)) `[PRIMARY]` — materially worse than LS. Assume a forced migration within the product's life; read that blog post manually before committing.

---

# TOPIC A — THE HYBRID QUESTION

## A1. What share picks web when both are offered

**The only controlled experiment that exists: RevenueCat's Dipsea A/B test.** ([revenuecat.com/blog/growth/iap-vs-web-purchases-conversion-test](https://www.revenuecat.com/blog/growth/iap-vs-web-purchases-conversion-test/), run **7–28 May 2025**, pub. 14 May, upd. 28 May 2025) `[VENDOR — but a real controlled test, disclosed sample, on RevenueCat's own app, and the result cuts AGAINST their web-billing product. Unusually credible.]`

- **Sample: ~12,500 users, ~3,100 per variant, 3 weeks, US.**
- **Variant C (both offered, web discounted 30%): "Roughly 36% of revenue in Variant C came via web purchases."** ← the answer to your question, **but note the web option was 30% cheaper.** At price parity the share would be lower.

| Variant | Initial conv. | Trial start | Trial→paid | Paid conv. |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B — IAP only | **27.0%** | 25.6% | 25.0% | 6.3% |
| C — IAP + discounted web | **23.5%** | 22.0% | **28.2%** | 5.9% |
| D — Web only | **18.1%** | 15.9% | 26.3% | 5.3% |

**The single most decision-relevant number in this entire brief:** **70.9% of users who tapped the IAP button completed. Only 44.4% of users who tapped the web link completed.** → **~56% of people who tap an external purchase link never finish.**

**Net proceeds per customer:**

| Variant | @ 30% Apple fee | @ 15% SBP fee |
|---|---|---|
| B (IAP) | $2.09 | **$2.53** |
| C (IAP+web) | $1.94 | $2.17 |
| D (Web) | $1.96 | $1.96 |

**At the 15% SBP rate, IAP-only beat both hybrid and web-only by 17% and 29% respectively.** Even at the full 30% rate, web returned 94¢ on the dollar.

**Caveat I'd stress:** n=1 app, one category (audio erotica — unusually privacy-sensitive, where users may actively *prefer* Apple's opaque billing line-item), 3 weeks, US only. Do not treat 27/18 as universal.

**Ecosystem-level share (RevenueCat *State of Subscription Apps 2026*, 115,000 apps / $16B / 1B+ transactions, primarily CY2025):** ([revenuecat.com/state-of-subscription-apps](https://www.revenuecat.com/state-of-subscription-apps)) `[VENDOR]`
- **Web = 3.2% of subscription revenue globally; 4.9% in North America; 0.8% in IN/SEA.**
- **41% of Tier 5 (top-decile) apps have web revenue vs 1.3% of Tier 1 (hobby) apps — a 31× gap.**
- Read: **web checkout is a behaviour of already-successful apps, added on top of a working IAP business — not a launch strategy.**

**Superwall** ([superwall.com/blog/initial-data-is-in-app-to-web-conversion-rates-after-the-app-store-ruling](https://superwall.com/blog/initial-data-is-in-app-to-web-conversion-rates-after-the-app-store-ruling), 7 May 2025) `[VENDOR]` — **no sample size, no date range disclosed:**
- Initial conversion **−11.9%**; net proceeds **+19.85%** — *but that assumes the 30% rate*.
- **Explicitly for SBP developers: "Moving to web payments could actually reduce your net proceeds by about 1.30%."** Only turns positive if trial conversion improves ~20% (then +17.74%).
- **No 2026 follow-up published.** I looked.

**Adapty and Appcharge:** both published prescriptive guides with **zero proprietary data**. ([adapty.io](https://adapty.io/blog/new-us-ruling-on-external-ios-payments/) 25 Aug 2025; [appcharge.com](https://www.appcharge.com/blog/ios-external-payments-what-actually-works) 17 Aug 2025) `[VENDOR]`

**Paddle's aggregate** ([paddle.com/resources/external-payments-guide-ios-app-developers](https://www.paddle.com/resources/external-payments-guide-ios-app-developers), 2026) `[VENDOR — "aggregated from Paddle customers &amp; RevenueCat content", no methodology]`: net proceeds "24%+ good / 15% average (SBP)"; conversion change "0–1%+ good / 5–12% down average"; LTV "+17% good / +10% average". Named: Stoikk +25% est. revenue; Runna 15% higher web retention; HubX $100k recovered in 72 days; Nexus Mods 9× uplift in China. **Treat as marketing.**

## A2. Incremental or cannibalising?

**Cannibalising, on the only controlled evidence.** RevenueCat's own 12-month retrospective ([revenuecat.com/blog/growth/why-you-shouldnt-use-app-to-web](https://www.revenuecat.com/blog/growth/why-you-shouldnt-use-app-to-web), pub. **14 Apr 2026**) `[VENDOR, arguing against their own product]`:

| | IAP only | IAP + web | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conversion to paying | 7.8% | 7.4% | **−5.1%** |
| Active subscribers | 211 | 209 | −1% |
| **Set to renew** | 46 | **101** | **+119%** |
| Churned | 121 | 104 | −15% |
| **Refunded customers** | 28 | **38** | **+35.7%** |

The "+119% set to renew" is the strongest pro-web number anywhere — but RevenueCat themselves attribute it to **users forgetting to cancel**, not satisfaction. Corroborating: among **2,000 active subscriptions started in May [2025]**, **2.5% of web subscribers had disabled auto-renewal vs 18% of App Store subscribers.** The **+35.7% refunds** is the cost of that.

**Ecosystem check — no visible cannibalisation at scale.** US App Store consumer spending **grew 18.1% to $55.5B in 2025**; global **$155.8B, +21.6%** — the first full year US link-outs were free. ([Appfigures via TechCrunch, 14 Jan 2026](https://techcrunch.com/2026/01/14/app-downloads-declined-again-in-2025-but-consumer-spending-soared-to-nearly-156b/)) `[IND]` Sensor Tower's *State of Mobile 2026* doesn't mention external payments at all.

**Do not conflate app-to-web with web2app.** FunnelFox's *State of Web2App 2026* claims web funnels get **2× conversion and 1.5× LTV** ([funnelfox.com](https://funnelfox.com/state-of-web2app-2026)) `[VENDOR, no sample/methodology disclosed]` — but that's **paid ads → web landing page → checkout before install**, a different flow with a selection effect. It is not evidence about link-outs. Airbridge (`[VENDOR]`) makes the sharper point: web-to-app "destroys value when it processes traffic that would have converted natively — you will lose 25–35% of conversions."

## A3. What real companies MEASURED and said after 30 Apr 2025

**Who moved within days:** Spotify (approved 2 May 2025 — first), Kindle/Amazon, Patreon (6 May), Fortnite/Epic (submitted 9 May). ([9to5Mac](https://9to5mac.com/2025/05/02/spotify-external-links-app-update-approved/), [TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2025/05/06/patreons-app-can-now-accept-web-payments-after-u-s-app-store-changes/)) `[IND]`

| Company | What they disclosed | Source |
|---|---|---|
| **Match Group** | **~$14M savings Q4 2025; ~$90M in 2026** from alternative payments. Then raised: **"We now think it will contribute about $130 million of savings for 2026, that is about $20 million better than we expected."** — CFO Gary Bailey. Also confirmed **"Zero percent commissions on alternative payments"** still in effect, resolution expected "later this year, early next year." **No adoption % disclosed.** | [PR, 4 Nov 2025](https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/match-group-announces-third-quarter-results-302604631.html) + [Q2 2026 call, 11 Aug 2026](https://www.fool.com/earnings/call-transcripts/2026/08/11/match-group-mtch-q2-2026-earnings-call-transcript/) `[PRIMARY]` |
| **Bumble** | CFO Kevin Cook, Q4 2025: **"direct payments contributed approximately one percentage point of year over year gross margin expansion"**; **"we have already got more than half of our U.S. iOS payments being made through Apple Pay"**; **"We have not seen any friction… there could be some impact on revenue. There has not been to date"**; **"there seems to be an improvement in renewals… which was somewhat unexpected."** Q1 2026 GM **+300bps**; Q2 2026 GM **+380bps YoY**, cost of revenue 26% vs 29%. | [Q4 2025 call, 11 Mar 2026](https://www.fool.com/earnings/call-transcripts/2026/03/11/bumble-bmbl-q4-2025-earnings-call-transcript/) `[PRIMARY]` |
| **Spotify** | Amicus brief, 21 May 2025: **"Spotify's internal data show that its iOS app updates have already resulted in a significant increase in iOS users upgrading to a Premium subscription."** Conversion up "substantially" vs Android "relatively constant" over two weeks. **Explicitly declined to give numbers.** Q2 2026: record 33.4% gross margin, 300M subs — **not attributed to external payments.** | [TechCrunch, 21 May 2025](https://techcrunch.com/2025/05/21/spotify-says-support-for-external-payments-on-ios-has-already-boosted-subscriptions/) `[IND reporting a PRIMARY filing]` |
| **Epic** | Boosted **Epic Rewards from 5% → 20%** cashback for using Epic's payment system, permanent for Epic's own games, across iOS/Android/PC/web. **No IAP purchases earn rewards.** No outcome data published. | [store.epicgames.com](https://store.epicgames.com/en-US/news/better-deals-in-fortnite-and-more-players-get-20-back-in-epic-rewards-starting-today) `[PRIMARY]` |
| **Duolingo** | **Nothing disclosed.** Searched Q1/Q2 2026 calls and 8-Ks — no external-payments breakout. | — |
| **Patreon, Kindle, Blinkist, Tinder/Hinge individually** | **No app-level numbers published by any of them.** | — |
| **Delivery apps** | **Irrelevant to this decision** — physical goods and real-world services have *always* been exempt from IAP, worldwide. | Apple Guidelines 3.1.3 `[PRIMARY]` |

**Sell-side context** (Benzinga/analyst, 2 Jul 2025) `[IND]`: app store fees are **17% of Duolingo's cost of revenue, 20% each for Match and Bumble**. Every **500bp** shift to off-app payments → EBITDA **+3.3% / +2.8% / +3.0%** respectively.

**Read across:** the companies with disclosed numbers are all doing **hundreds of millions in revenue with in-house checkout, their own card-on-file, Apple Pay, and dunning teams.** Bumble's "&gt;half of US iOS payments" is the best "share picks web" datapoint outside Dipsea — but it's achieved with resources a solo dev doesn't have.

## A4. Adoption-rate reporting from analytics firms

**Honest answer: no public market-wide count exists.** I searched Appfigures, Sensor Tower, TechCrunch, The Verge, MacRumors, 9to5Mac and Daring Fireball. **Nobody has published "N apps now use external purchase links."** Best proxy is RevenueCat's 3.2% global / 4.9% NA web revenue share.

What the outlets *did* publish:
- **Appfigures, 2 May 2025** — *"Should App Developers Switch to Web Payments?"* Conclusion: **"the economics don't support widespread adoption of external payments"** and **"bypassing Apple sounds good, but for most, it's a trap."** Reasoning: once you absorb processing, fraud, chargebacks, tax compliance and conversion friction, **"you'll end up not very far off from what Apple is charging."** ([appfigures.com/resources/insights/20250502](https://appfigures.com/resources/insights/20250502?f=5)) `[IND — closest thing to a neutral analyst view]`
- **Daring Fireball, 15 May 2025** — on the Dipsea 28%/18% result: unsurprising, **"IAP really is more convenient"**; Apple built a system that doesn't need exclusivity to win. ([daringfireball.net](https://daringfireball.net/linked/2025/05/15/revenuecat-external-purchase-report)) `[IND]`

**No indie/solo-dev public disclosure with real numbers exists.** I searched specifically (blogs, Reddit, Indie Hackers) and found none. **Every number in Topic A is either a vendor's own experiment or a public company at $100M+ scale.** That gap is itself a finding.

---

# TOPIC B — LEGAL / POLICY STATE AS OF AUG 2026

## B1. Epic v. Apple — timeline and current status

| Date | Event |
|---|---|
| **30 Apr 2025** | Judge Gonzalez Rogers finds Apple in **willful civil contempt**; bars **any** commission on US external-link purchases; kills the scare screen. `[IND]` |
| **1 May 2025** | Apple updates Guidelines 3.1.1, 3.1.1(a), 3.1.3, 3.1.3(a) for the US storefront. ([Apple Developer News](https://developer.apple.com/news/?id=9txfddzf)) `[PRIMARY]` |
| **Jun 2025** | Ninth Circuit denies Apple's emergency stay. |
| **11 Dec 2025** | **Ninth Circuit affirms contempt but REVERSES the blanket commission ban.** Apple may charge fees limited to *"the costs that are genuinely and reasonably necessary for its coordination of external links… but no more"* — plus limited IP compensation, **excluding** security/privacy costs. Court called the district court's approach *"blunt force to ban all commissions"* and an abuse of discretion. Also lets Apple **restrict external links from being more prominent than IAP buttons** (font/size/placement), while requiring at-least-parity. ([9th Cir. opinion](https://cdn.ca9.uscourts.gov/datastore/opinions/2025/12/11/25-2935.pdf), [MacRumors](https://www.macrumors.com/2025/12/11/apple-app-store-fees-external-payment-links/), [Shinder Cantor Lerner](https://scl-llp.com/ninth-circuit-upholds-apple-contempt-finding-but-narrows-scope-of-remedial-relief/)) `[PRIMARY + IND]` |
| **30 Mar 2026** | En banc rehearing denied. |
| **6 May 2026** | **SCOTUS (Kagan) denies Apple's emergency stay** of the mandate. ([9to5Mac](https://9to5mac.com/2026/05/06/supreme-court-rejects-apples-stay-request-epic-games-case-to-head-back-to-district-court/)) `[IND]` |
| **30 Jun 2026** | **SCOTUS grants certiorari** on the contempt finding. ([IPWatchdog](https://ipwatchdog.com/2026/06/30/high-court-grants-cert-in-apples-challenge-to-ninth-circuit-contempt-ruling-in-app-store-dispute/)) `[IND]` |
| **11 Aug 2026** | Judge Rogers denies Apple's bid to delay the fee proceedings pending SCOTUS. |
| **12–13 Aug 2026** | SCOTUS grants a **24-hour** stay, then denies further delay. |
| **13–14 Aug 2026** | **Apple files its proposed rates.** Epic gets **60 days** to respond before a hearing. Apple's SCOTUS brief due **14 Sep 2026**. |

### Answer to your question: **Yes — 0% is still in force in the US today.**

Confirmed independently by Match Group's CFO on 11 Aug 2026: *"Zero percent commissions on alternative payments"* continue while litigation proceeds; resolution expected *"later this year, early next year."* `[PRIMARY]`

**But the 0% is a temporary artefact of an unresolved remedy, not a settled rule.** The Ninth Circuit already held Apple is *entitled* to something. Apple has now proposed:

| Tier | Proposed US link-out rate |
|---|---|
| Standard | **15%** |
| Video/News/Mini Apps Partner Programs + **subscription renewals** | **10%** |
| **Small Business Program** | **5%** |

Apple's justification: cost-only commissions *"fail to account for its investments in developer tools and IP-protected technologies."* Epic's counter: under the Ninth Circuit's "necessary costs" definition **the correct number is 0%**, and Apple's proposal is *"far outside of the bounds"* of the court's guidance. ([TechCrunch 14 Aug](https://techcrunch.com/2026/08/14/apple-proposes-to-take-a-15-cut-of-purchases-made-outside-the-app-store/), [MacRumors 13 Aug](https://www.macrumors.com/2026/08/13/app-store-fees-apple-link-outs/), [Michael Tsai roundup 17 Aug](https://mjtsai.com/blog/2026/08/17/apple-proposes-15-external-purchase-fee/)) `[IND]`

**Strong signal 15% is Apple's global anchor:** Apple settled with Brazil's CADE in Dec 2025 at **15% steering commission**, and Japan's MSCA implementation (18 Dec 2025) uses **15% (10% for program participants) on purchases within 7 days of a link tap**. ([MacRumors 17 Dec 2025](https://www.macrumors.com/2025/12/17/japan-app-store-feature-updates/), [9to5Mac](https://9to5mac.com/2025/12/17/apple-announces-sweeping-app-store-and-iphone-changes-in-japan/)) `[IND]`

## B2. Current commission rates by region

**US (today):** **0%** on link-outs. **No entitlement required, no disclosure sheet.** Apple's own Guideline 3.1.1(a), verbatim: *"These entitlements are not required for developers to include buttons, external links, or other calls to action in their United States storefront apps."* Same carve-out added to 3.1.3(a) for reader apps. ([developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/)) `[PRIMARY]`

&gt; ⚠️ **Several 2026 "implementation guides" are wrong on this.** [stora.sh (16 May 2026)](https://stora.sh/blog/2026-05-16-apple-app-store-external-purchase-links-implementation-guide) states the US requires the `com.developer.storekit.external-purchase-link` entitlement, a system disclosure sheet, and 27%/12% commission. **All three are stale/incorrect for the US storefront.** `[WEAK]` Use Apple's guidelines page.

**EU (post-1 Jan 2026):** Core Technology **Fee** (€0.50/install) eliminated, replaced by **Core Technology Commission 5%**. Stack: **2% initial acquisition** (first 6 months, new users; 0% for SBP) + **Store Services Tier 1 5% / Tier 2 13% (10% SBP)** + **CTC 5%**.
- **SBP with full services ≈ 17% · SBP Tier-1 only ≈ 10% · standard full services ≈ 20%**, plus payment processing.
- ([RevenueCat, 27 Jun 2025](https://www.revenuecat.com/blog/growth/apple-eu-dma-update-june-2025/); [FunnelFox, 10 Apr 2026](https://blog.funnelfox.com/apple-app-store-fees-2026-eu-dma/)) `[VENDOR — Apple's own EU support page is the authority; verify before relying]`

**Japan (from 18 Dec 2025, terms deadline 17 Mar 2026):** 15% link-out (10% for programs), **7-day attribution**. Unlike the US, Japan **requires side-by-side IAP** whenever you offer an alternative. **Brazil:** 15%, per Dec 2025 CADE settlement.

## B3. Small Business Program in 2026 — unchanged

From Apple's page and Schedule 2 §3.4(b), v126 (17 Dec 2025) `[PRIMARY]`:
- **15%** on paid apps and IAP. Threshold **$1,000,000 USD in proceeds** (net of Apple's commission and certain taxes) in the prior calendar year, **aggregated across all Associated Developer Accounts**.
- Exceed it mid-year → standard rate on future sales. Fall back below → requalify the year after. **Annual re-enrolment required.**
- Adjustment takes effect **15 days after the end of the fiscal month** in which enrolment is approved.
- **I found no 2025 or 2026 changes to the program.** ([developer.apple.com/app-store/small-business-program](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/small-business-program/))
- Also still in §3.4(a): **standard 30% drops to 15% on auto-renewing subscriptions after the customer accrues &gt;1 year of paid service** — so retained subscribers are 15% regardless of SBP.
- **Note:** the $1M threshold counts **App Store proceeds only.** Web revenue does not count against it.

## B4. Reader apps and linking out — 2026

Reader-app rules are unchanged globally, **except** the US carve-out now added to 3.1.3(a): the External Link Account Entitlement **is not required** for US storefront reader apps.

**Two enforcement facts that matter more to a solo dev than the rates:**

1. **Guideline 3.1.1 still requires IAP alongside external links.** You cannot drop IAP for a non-reader app, even in the US.
2. **Links must open in the system browser, not an in-app browser.** Apple made Patreon change exactly this within ten days of shipping (6 → 16 May 2025). ([TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2025/05/16/after-adding-its-own-billing-option-on-ios-apple-asks-patreon-to-move-it-to-an-external-browser)) `[IND]`

**Apple is still actively policing this in 2026.** **Cal AI** (~$50M ARR, owned by MyFitnessPal) was **pulled from the App Store in April 2026** under Guideline **3.1.1** (embedded in-app Stripe flow with IAP removed entirely), **3.1.2c** (weekly price shown more prominently than the actual charge, unclear auto-renewal disclosure), and **Code of Conduct 5.6** (second subscription prompt after the user declined the first). Reinstated after ~4 days. ([TechCrunch, 21 Apr 2026](https://techcrunch.com/2026/04/21/apples-cal-ai-crackdown-signals-its-still-policing-the-app-store/), [MacRumors](https://www.macrumors.com/2026/04/21/apple-cal-ai-app-store-removal/)) `[IND]` — **For a solo dev with one app and no legal budget, a 4-day removal is an extinction-level event.**

---

# TOPIC C — NON-FINANCIAL TRADE-OFFS

## C1. Customer email — Apple gives you nothing that identifies a human

| Surface | What you actually get |
|---|---|
| **Sales &amp; Trends Subscriber Report** | Event date, app/sub IDs, duration, offer fields, price, proceeds, **country**, **device**, and a **"randomly generated Subscriber ID that's unique to each customer and developer."** It **resets after 180 days of lapse** and on app transfer. **No name, no email, no Apple ID.** ([App Store Connect Help](https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/reference/subscriber-report)) `[PRIMARY]` |
| **App Store Server API / Notifications** | `transactionId`, `originalTransactionId`, `appTransactionId` (unique per family member), `appAccountToken` (a UUID **you** generate — your identifier, not Apple's, and **unavailable for family-shared transactions**). WWDC25 added `appAccountToken` to `JWSRenewalInfo` plus a Set-App-Account-Token endpoint. **No PII.** ([WWDC25 session 249](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2025/249/)) `[PRIMARY]` |
| **Customer Reviews** | Nickname + text. No contact channel. |
| **Any "share contact info" program** | **Does not exist for consumer IAP.** |

**Nothing changed in 2025 or 2026.** The 2026 changes are SDK minimums (iOS 26 SDK from 28 Apr 2026), medical-device declarations, age ratings, and 100+ new App Analytics metrics — all aggregate, with **differential privacy** on peer benchmarks.

**The 2021 developer-settlement is about permission, not data.** Apple confirmed developers *may* email users about outside payment options — but only using **contact info the user gave the app directly**, with consent and opt-out. Apple supplies nothing. ([apple.com/newsroom, 26 Aug 2021](https://www.apple.com/newsroom/2021/08/apple-us-developers-agree-to-app-store-updates/)) `[PRIMARY]`

**Sign in with Apple relay is a leaky partial workaround:** `&lt;random&gt;@privaterelay.appleid.com`; you must **register and authenticate sending domains** with Apple or mail is dropped; **hard cap 100 messages/day per relay address**; the user can kill forwarding permanently at any time. **Not a portable asset** — you cannot export it to a new ESP without re-registering. ([support.apple.com/en-us/105078](https://support.apple.com/en-us/105078)) `[PRIMARY]`

**Can we quantify why an email list matters for a solo founder? Honestly, no.**
- **No published study exists** on email-list value for solo-founder consumer subscription apps.
- Best available is DTC ecommerce: **$4.40 median annual email-attributed revenue per subscriber** (portfolio aggregate $12.78; range $0.39–$144.96; 4.14% of list purchased; email drove a median 31% of total revenue). Sample: **834,474 subscribers across 15 DTC brands, May 2025–Apr 2026**. ([bsandco.us, 16 Mar 2026](https://bsandco.us/blog-post/subscriber-value-benchmarks)) `[VENDOR — agency selling email consulting off these numbers; DTC ecommerce, NOT apps]`
- The "36:1 email ROI" figure is DMA-lineage; Litmus's own research says 38:1, and their 2025 survey has marketers **self-reporting 10:1 to 36:1**. `[VENDOR, self-reported, not measured incrementality — near-worthless for a decision]`
- **Email vs push conversion: no credible head-to-head found.** Airship's 2025 benchmark report is gated and its landing page discloses no numbers. Circulating "push 28% vs email 1–2%" claims use incompatible denominators.

**The defensible argument is structural, not statistical:** push requires an OS opt-in you don't control and dies on uninstall; an owned email survives uninstall, platform migration, and Apple policy changes. **Real asset — but don't put a number on it, because the number doesn't exist for your case.**

## C2. Refunds

**Under IAP, Apple decides and you get a 5-field vote.**
- Customers have **~90 days** to request; EU adds a statutory 14-day cooling-off. Under the Paid Applications Agreement **Apple may refund and charge you back the full price.**
- Mechanism: `CONSUMPTION_REQUEST` → **Send Consumption Information**. WWDC25 cut this to **5 fields from 12** (3 mandatory: `customerConsented`, `sampleContentProvided`, `deliveryStatus`; 2 optional: `refundPreference` — now supporting **GRANT_PRORATED** — and `consumptionPercentage`). `consumptionRequestReason` now tells you why.
- Apple's own framing: *"The consumption data you submit **helps the App Store decide** to grant a full refund, prorate the refund, or decline it altogether."* ([Apple docs](https://developer.apple.com/documentation/appstoreserverapi/send-consumption-information)) `[PRIMARY]`
- **12-hour response window.** The claim that non-response **auto-approves** the refund comes only from refund-defence vendors and **could not be confirmed in Apple's docs.** `[UNVERIFIED]`
- **Net: you have influence, not control. Apple is adjudicator, counterparty, and appeals court.**

**Under an MoR, you keep discretion but still bear the cost.** From Lemon Squeezy's own docs `[VENDOR-PRIMARY]`:
- Refunds *"can be issued at any time via the Lemon Squeezy dashboard. **The refunded amount (minus our platform fee) will be deducted from your next payout**."* → **you decide**, unlike Apple.
- But: *"**Lemon Squeezy reserves the right to issue refunds within 60 days of purchase, at its own discretion**."*
- Chargebacks: *"we will offer a full refund on your behalf and deduct the refunded amount (minus our platform fee) **plus a $15 dispute fee** from your next payout… **If your store has too many chargebacks, our team may intervene and suspend your account**."*
- Buyer Terms: refunds at LS's *"sole discretion"*; buyers filing meritless chargebacks face **$100 liquidated damages**; governed by **Utah** law.
- **The nuance most comparisons miss: "MoR takes chargeback liability" means legal/processing liability and acquirer-relationship risk. The economic cost still flows to you via payout deduction, and excessive disputes still get you suspended.**

**Refund-rate data — App Store IAP is well measured:**
- **Typical mobile app refund rate: 2%–5% of paid transactions.** Basis: Adapty's analysis of **$1.9B revenue across 11,000+ apps** plus RevenueCat's 75,000+ app data. ([adapty.io, 10 Feb 2026](https://adapty.io/blog/refund-rate-metrics-and-benchmarking/)) `[VENDOR]`
- By duration: weekly **2.6%** · monthly **3.0–3.5%** · annual **4.2%**
- By category: **Education 4.86–5.1%** · **Health/Fitness 4.71%** · Productivity 2.5–3.5% · Business 2.88% · Gaming 2.67% · Shopping 1.5–2.5% · **Travel 1.51%**
- By region: **South Korea up to 10%** · LATAM &gt;3% · USA &gt;3% · W. Europe/APAC &lt;3%
- **⚠️ Relevant to a sleep app: Health/Fitness is the second-highest refund category at ~4.7%.**

**Refund rates for web checkout on digital subscriptions: NO PUBLIC DATA EXISTS.** I checked Paddle/ProfitWell, Lemon Squeezy, Recurly, Chargebee, Baremetrics. None publish it. **Any side-by-side IAP-vs-web refund comparison you encounter is fabricated.**

**Fraud/abuse:**
- **16% of consumers admit filing a false fraud claim** despite being satisfied. Survey: **1,075 US adults, Oct 2025**. **First-party fraud = 36% of all reported fraud in 2024** (up from 15%); **digital subscriptions flagged at 20% abuse.** ([sift.com Q4 2025 index](https://sift.com/index-reports-disputes-q4-2025/)) `[VENDOR, disclosed methodology]`
- Web chargebacks: **all-industry average 0.26% in Q3 2025, +24% YoY**; **B2C SaaS/services +83% YoY.** Same source.
- **Visa VAMP thresholds** — 2.20% at mid-2025 launch → **1.5% from 1 Oct 2025** → **0.9% "excessive" from 1 Jan 2026**, with **$10 per disputed transaction** above the limit. `[Widely and consistently reported, but I could not reach a Visa primary document — verify.]`
- **Apple blocked $2.2B in fraudulent App Store transactions in 2025** ($11.2B over six years); 1.1B fraudulent account creations rejected; 5.4M stolen cards blocked. ([apple.com/newsroom, May 2026](https://www.apple.com/newsroom/2026/05/the-app-store-stopped-over-2-point-2-billion-usd-in-fraudulent-transactions-in-2025/)) `[PRIMARY, self-serving]`
- **This is the strongest non-financial argument for IAP: Apple absorbs stolen-card fraud, account takeover, and acquirer-relationship risk entirely. On web you own all of it.**

## C3. Payout timing

| | Cadence | Lag | Minimum | FX |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Apple** | 12×/yr, one per **fiscal month** (4-4-5 calendar: two 28-day + one 35-day per quarter) | **~33 days after fiscal month close** | **$0.02** for USD/EUR/GBP/AUD/CAD/JPY/INR/SGD/CHF and most majors; **$40 for unlisted regions** | Apple sets regional prices, handles FX, remits in your bank currency |
| **Lemon Squeezy** | **Twice monthly** — created 1st &amp; 15th, paid 14th &amp; 28th; net sales held **13 days** | **~10–24 days** + 1–5 to land | **$50** (rolls over) | All processed in **USD** at mid-market, no checkout FX markup. **US bank free; non-US bank 1%; PayPal US $0.50; PayPal non-US 3% capped at $30** |
| **Paddle** | Monthly — created 1st, sent by 15th | **~15–45 days** | **$100** default | Free if payout currency matches your bank's; **$15 SWIFT** otherwise; **up to 1.5% conversion margin** |
| **Stripe direct** | Rolling daily/weekly/monthly | **2 business days** US; first payout 7–14 days | None | — |

**Critical caveats:**
- **Apple does not publish an official payout calendar.** RevenueCat, which maintains the community estimate, says outright: *"These dates are estimates, since Apple doesn't publish official payment schedules."* ([revenuecat.com, upd. 6 May 2026](https://www.revenuecat.com/blog/growth/apple-fiscal-calendar-year-payment-dates)) `[VENDOR]`
- **The real per-sale wait is 33 to ~68 days, not 33.** A sale on day 1 of a 35-day fiscal month waits 35 + 33 = 68. **Mean ≈ 48–50 days.** *(Derived arithmetic, not a sourced figure.)*
- **Apple has NOT moved to more frequent payouts.** No announcement found in 2025 or 2026.
- **Cash-flow delta: Apple ~48 days average vs Lemon Squeezy ~17 days.** Real but modest — and Apple's **$0.02 threshold is friendlier than LS's $50 or Paddle's $100** at launch scale.

## C4. Subscription mechanics — IAP wins this section decisively

**What Apple does for you, free** (all from [developer.apple.com/app-store/subscriptions](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/subscriptions/)) `[PRIMARY]`:
- **Billing Retry: 60 days**, ML-optimised retry timing. Recovery within 60 days preserves paid-service days.
- **Billing Grace Period: 3, 16, or 28 days**, configurable — access continues during retry. **Off by default; you must enable it.**
- **Billing Problem Message (iOS 16.4+):** a system sheet on app launch lets the customer fix their card **without leaving your app.** Zero engineering.
- **Win-back offers** surfaced automatically on the App Store product page, in editorial/recommendations, via an in-app sheet with **no work required**, in Subscription settings, and by direct URL — plus a Win-back Eligibility Report.
- **Price increases:** Apple auto-sends push + email + in-app messaging, tracks consent, lets you grandfather unlimited existing subscribers.
- **Family Sharing:** up to **5 family members** on one subscription. **Enabling is irreversible.** Costs you nothing directly — the cost is **revenue dilution (up to 6 users per subscription)** and identity plumbing (`appAccountToken` is unavailable for family transactions; you must use `appTransactionId`). **Apple publishes no data on its net revenue effect, and neither does anyone else.**
- **Constraint:** you **cannot intercept the cancel flow.** Apple owns the management sheet; you can only add surrounding UI.

**What web gives you:** your own portal, your own cancel flow with save offers/pause/downgrade (things Apple forbids), your own dunning. **No Family Sharing equivalent** — you'd build seat management. LS subscriptions accept **cards, Apple Pay, Google Pay, PayPal only** (one-time purchases get ~21 methods). LS charges **+5%** on payments recovered via abandoned-cart emails, and **publishes no dunning recovery rate**.

**INVOLUNTARY CHURN — the headline comparison:**

| | Involuntary share |
|---|---|
| **App Store** | **14% of subscription cancellations** are involuntary billing failures (improved from 15.1%) |
| **Google Play** | **31%** (worsened from 28.2%) |
| **Web/card subscription businesses generally** | **20%–40% of total churn** (up to 68% in subscription boxes) |

Sources: RevenueCat *SOSA 2026*, **115,000+ apps / $16B / 1B+ transactions, primarily CY2025** ([revenuecat.com](https://www.revenuecat.com/state-of-subscription-apps)) `[VENDOR]`; Recurly, [30 Apr 2026](https://recurly.com/blog/failed-payment-recovery-data-based-strategy/) `[VENDOR]`.

RevenueCat's own explanation for why iOS is half of Android: *"more of the recovery happens automatically through billing retry and the Billing Problem Message feature."*

&gt; ⚠️ **Denominators differ** — RevenueCat measures share of *cancellations*, Recurly share of *churn* across a mixed B2C/B2B, physical+digital portfolio. Also, RevenueCat's blog says **15.2% / 32.3%** where the report page says **14% / ~31%**; use "~14–15% on iOS" and note the inconsistency.

Recurly dunning benchmarks `[VENDOR]`: baseline recovery **~53%**, optimised **~71%**; **90% of recoveries happen within 10 days**; recovered subscribers stay **7 more months**. Their "$129B industry loss" figure is a marketing extrapolation from their own customer lift — **weak, discard**.

**Gap: no published recovery rate for Apple's billing retry specifically.** The "grace period recovers 15–20% more" line circulating online traces to an SEO blog with no data — **do not use it**. RevenueCat's real 15–20% figure is about **Android dunning**, not Apple grace periods.

**This is probably the strongest operational argument for IAP in the whole brief: Apple's stored credential + ML retry + 60-day window + Billing Problem Message + grace period is better involuntary-churn infrastructure than a solo dev will build or buy.**

## C5. Trust / conversion

**The only real measurement is the CTA-completion gap: 70.9% (IAP) vs 44.4% (web link).** That's the trust-and-friction penalty, measured — though it does not isolate *cause* from *friction*.

**Supporting:**
- **Baymard Institute: average documented cart abandonment 70.22%** — meta-analysis of **50 published studies**, updated **22 Sep 2025**. Mobile 73–75%. ([baymard.com/lists/cart-abandonment-rate](https://baymard.com/lists/cart-abandonment-rate)) `[IND — the field's canonical figure]`
- **Apple Pay materially offsets web friction.** Stripe ran a **statistically significant holdback experiment** across 50+ payment methods: businesses offering **Apple Pay saw +22.3% conversion and +22.5% revenue on average**. ([stripe.com/blog/testing-the-conversion-impact-of-50-plus-global-payment-methods](https://stripe.com/blog/testing-the-conversion-impact-of-50-plus-global-payment-methods)) `[VENDOR, but a disclosed holdback design at large scale — the most rigorous checkout evidence found]` Lemon Squeezy supports Apple Pay on subscriptions, so this lever is available. Bumble's "&gt;half of US iOS payments via Apple Pay" corroborates it in the wild.
- Claims of "+58% mobile conversion with Apple Pay" trace to content-marketing with no methodology. `[WEAK — don't use]`
- **Sign in with Apple works on web** via Apple's JS SDK, but **I could not verify Lemon Squeezy supports it** either way.

**Is there survey data showing consumers trust Apple billing more than an unknown web checkout? No. I searched specifically and found none.**

## C6. VAT / sales tax — what an MoR actually removes

**What Apple handles.** Exhibit B §1 to Schedule 2 &amp; 3, **dated 29 Jan 2026** `[PRIMARY]`: *"If taxes apply, Apple shall collect and remit to the competent tax authorities the taxes described in Section 3.2…"* — followed by a list of **91 territories**, including **all 27 EU states, the UK, the US, Canada, Australia, NZ, Norway, Switzerland, Japan, India, Korea, Singapore, Taiwan, Türkiye, South Africa, Saudi Arabia, UAE**, and most of LatAm.

Apple is legally **agent** in the US/CA/AU/NZ/JP/LatAm and **commissionaire** ("an agent who purports to act on their own behalf and concludes agreements in their own name but acts on behalf of other persons") elsewhere — which is the mechanism by which **Apple, not you, is the EU/UK VAT registrant.**

Carve-outs: **China** is excluded (yours). **Japan, Russia, Korea, Singapore, Indonesia, Philippines, Thailand and others are marked "solely applicable to non-resident Developers"** — favourable to a US dev. Exhibit B §2: for any territory not listed, *"You shall be solely responsible."*

&gt; ⚠️ **Apple's marketing pages contradict its own contract** — one says "64 of those territories," another "over 70." **Exhibit B lists 91. Trust the agreement.**

**Does the developer owe US state sales tax on App Store sales? No.** Two-part confirmation: (1) "United States" is expressly in the Exhibit B §1 list `[PRIMARY]`; (2) the mechanism is state marketplace-facilitator law post-*Wayfair* — the [Streamlined Sales Tax Governing Board's Marketplace Seller guidance](https://www.streamlinedsalestax.org/for-businesses/marketplace-sellers) answers "No" for the large majority of states. **Caveat: NE, NJ, SD and WA can still require registration and zero returns in some circumstances, and every state's answer changes the moment you also sell direct.**

**What you'd face WITHOUT an MoR (plain Stripe direct):**

| Jurisdiction | Threshold for a US-established solo dev |
|---|---|
| **EU (27)** | **€0.** The €10,000 relief (Art. 59c) requires being *"established in only one Member State"* — you can't qualify. Register for **non-Union OSS** in any one member state, **quarterly filings** (30 Apr / 31 Jul / 31 Oct / 31 Jan), **10-year record retention** (Art. 63c). ([EC OSS portal](https://vat-one-stop-shop.ec.europa.eu/)) `[PRIMARY]` |
| **UK** | **£0.** HMRC VAT Notice 700/1, **updated 5 Aug 2026** `[PRIMARY]`, verbatim: *"**A registration threshold does not apply to non-established taxable persons, so you must register if you make taxable supplies of any value in the UK.**"* Drags in Making Tax Digital. |
| **India** | **£0/₹0** — OIDAR registration required *"irrespective of threshold limit."* **Monthly GSTR-5A**, 18% IGST, no input credit, requires an Indian authorised representative. ([GST portal](https://tutorial.gst.gov.in/userguide/registration/Non-Resident_Online_Services_Provider_manual.htm)) `[PRIMARY]` |
| **South Korea** | **Zero.** Simplified NTS registration within 20 days. |
| **Switzerland** | **CHF 100,000 WORLDWIDE turnover** + any Swiss B2C sale → requires a **Swiss fiscal representative** and often a **bond ~3% of projected turnover**. The nastiest of the set. |
| **Australia** | A$75,000 · **Canada** C$30,000 · **New Zealand** NZ$60,000 · **Norway** NOK 50,000 · **Japan** ¥10M · **Singapore** S$100k + S$1M global |
| **US states** | 45 states + DC have economic nexus; dominant threshold **$100,000**; outliers MS $250k, **TX $500k**, **NY $500k + 100 sales**. Transaction-count thresholds are being deleted (CO 2019, IN 2024, **IL 1 Jan 2026**) — helps low-price/high-volume sellers. |

**Realistically for a solo dev:** US state sales tax is the *least* of the problems — $100k into a single state implies $1–2M of US revenue. **The binding constraints are EU, UK, India and Korea, all at zero.**

**Cost of DIY.** European Commission SWD(2022) 393 final, 8 Dec 2022 `[PRIMARY — the EU costing its own regime]`: *"the minimum one-off cost of obtaining a VAT registration in another Member State is **EUR 1,200**"* and *"The minimum ongoing cost, on a yearly basis… is **EUR 8,000 for an average business** and **EUR 2,400 for a SME**."* **Read this correctly:** these are *per-Member-State* figures for individual registration — **OSS collapses 27 into one.** Honest DIY-with-OSS ≈ **~€1,200 one-off + ~€2,400/yr** for the whole EU, plus a separate UK stream, plus each other country as you cross its threshold.

**The real tail risk isn't the fine — it's OSS exclusion.** Three consecutive missed returns triggers exclusion plus a **two-year quarantine** from all OSS schemes, during which selling to the EU legally requires **27 separate national registrations.** *That* is what turns €2,400 into €65,000. `[SECONDARY — Art. 58b of Reg. 282/2011, not read verbatim]`

**Tax software does NOT make you not-the-registrant.** Stripe Tax: **0.5% of processed volume** (pay-as-you-go) or **$90–$1,500/mo** (Tax Complete); **non-US registrations are brokered by Taxually at $150–$1,050 per country**; international filings **$45–$4,745**. ([support.stripe.com](https://support.stripe.com/questions/understanding-stripe-tax-pricing)) `[VENDOR-PRIMARY]` **Your name is on the EU OSS number, the UK VRN, the Indian GSTIN. The audit letter, the penalty assessment, and the 10-year record duty are yours.** Also: **"Stripe Tax is the only tax solution compatible with Managed Payments"** — Avalara/TaxJar/Quaderno are off the table there. `[PRIMARY]`

**Enforcement reality — be honest with yourself:** **I found no documented enforcement action against a solo/indie non-EU digital seller.** The legal obligation is unambiguous and starts at €0. The practical enforcement risk against a sub-$500k US sole trader appears empirically low. **But undisclosed multi-year VAT exposure is a classic acquisition-killer in diligence, and the risk profile changes the moment you grow, raise, or sell.**

One practitioner datapoint ([HN, Jul 2024](https://news.ycombinator.com/item?id=41080520)) `[SECONDARY, self-reported]`: a UK founder spent *"a couple of days per month"* on tax paperwork without an MoR vs *"a couple of invoices per month"* with one. The widely circulated "4–8 hours per quarter for OSS" figure has **no traceable source — don't cite it.**

---

# WHERE THE DATA DOES NOT EXIST

1. **No market-wide count** of how many App Store apps use external purchase links. Not from Appfigures, Sensor Tower, or anyone.
2. **No refund-rate benchmark for web-checkout digital subscriptions** — so no valid IAP-vs-web refund comparison exists anywhere.
3. **No published recovery rate for Apple's billing retry.**
4. **No survey data** on consumers trusting Apple billing over unknown web checkout.
5. **No quantified value of Family Sharing** to a developer.
6. **No indie/solo-dev public disclosure** with real link-out numbers. Every Topic A figure is either a vendor's own experiment or a $100M+ public company.
7. **No email-list value data specific to consumer subscription apps.**
8. **Lemon Squeezy's "2026 Update" post body is unread** (client-side rendered, 403) — any claim about a migration deadline is unverified. **Read it manually.**
9. **Stripe Managed Payments GA-vs-preview status unconfirmed**; competitor-sourced pricing claims about it contradict Stripe's own page and should be discarded.

---

# WHAT THE EVIDENCE ACTUALLY SUPPORTS

| Dimension | Winner | Evidence strength |
|---|---|---|
| Checkout conversion | **IAP**, decisively — 70.9% vs 44.4% CTA completion | Best single datapoint; n=1 app, 3 weeks |
| Net revenue at the **15% SBP rate** | **IAP** — $2.53 vs $2.17 (hybrid) vs $1.96 (web-only) per customer | Same experiment; Superwall independently estimates **−1.30%** for SBP devs |
| Involuntary churn | **IAP** — 14–15% of cancellations vs 20–40% of web churn | Good; denominators differ |
| Fraud/chargeback exposure | **IAP** — Apple absorbs it all | Strong directionally; no paired rates exist |
| Tax compliance | **Tie** — both Apple and a good MoR fully remove it | Strong `[PRIMARY]` on both sides |
| Owning customer email | **Web**, decisively | Strong on mechanism; **value unquantified** |
| Refund discretion | **Web** — you decide vs Apple's 5-field, 12-hour vote | Strong `[PRIMARY]` both sides |
| Cancel-flow / pricing control | **Web** — Apple forbids interception | Strong `[PRIMARY]` |
| Payout speed | **Web** — ~17 vs ~48 days average | Strong; Apple's $0.02 threshold beats LS's $50 at launch |
| Platform risk | **IAP** — LS is converging into Stripe MP on an unannounced timeline | Direction strong, **no dates published** |

**The honest synthesis:** the case for web checkout here is **not financial and not conversion.** At the 15% SBP rate, the ~33% loss in trial starts plausibly makes web checkout **net revenue-negative** — before Apple's proposed 5% SBP link-out fee, before you own chargebacks and dunning, and before a forced Lemon Squeezy migration. And the fee gap is 4.5pts (US, monthly) or 1.6pts (EU, monthly), not 10pts — because Apple charges on the tax-exclusive base and Lemon Squeezy's real rate is 5.5–7% + $0.50.

**The case for web is ownership:** a portable email list, refund discretion, a cancel flow you control, and not building your only revenue channel on a fee structure currently before the Supreme Court. Whether that's worth a third of your trial starts is a judgment call, not a calculation.

**The pricing note that matters for this specific project:** on a **one-time $75/$100 licence**, the MoR advantage is **~8.5% vs 15%** — decisive. On a **low-ticket monthly subscription**, it nearly vanishes. **Ticket size should drive this decision more than anything else in this brief.**

**Watch the docket.** Epic has 60 days from ~14 Aug 2026 to respond to Apple's rate proposal; Apple's SCOTUS brief is due 14 Sep 2026; the merits ruling likely lands in 2027.</result>

## Claude · 2026-08-17T20:42:01.675Z

That research came back enormous. Three things from it actually change something for you; the rest I'll file.

**1. Your ticket size makes the fee gap decisive.** On a $9.99/month subscription, merchant-of-record vs IAP is only ~4.5 points apart — Apple charges on the tax-*exclusive* price while MoRs charge on the tax-*inclusive* total. But on a **$75–100 annual licence it's ~8.5% vs 15%**. Your pricing is exactly the case where web checkout wins clearly.

**2. The conversion penalty mostly doesn't apply to you.** The "IAP converts far better" data (70.9% vs 44.4% completion) measures people tapping a link *inside an app* and being thrown to a browser. Your buyers arrive at a website and are already in a browser. There's no context switch to lose them to.

**3. Stripe direct has a real catch.** The EU, UK, India and Korea all have a **zero** VAT threshold for non-established sellers. Your first €1 sale to Germany creates an obligation. That's what a merchant of record was buying you.

## Straight answer

**Stripe, and restrict sales to the US to start.** Stripe lets you limit which countries can check out. US-only means no EU VAT, no UK registration, no OSS filing — and US state sales tax doesn't bite until $100k into a single state, which implies $1–2M of revenue.

Your launch cohort is Wesleyan students anyway.

Then when your passport is renewed and there's revenue worth the trouble, move to Lemon Squeezy or Paddle and open up internationally. Migrating billing providers with 50 customers is a morning's work; with 5,000 it's a project.

**The Stripe webhook is already built and waiting.** Sign up, create the four products, point the webhook at `https://trysleepwell.app/api/stripe/webhook`, send me the two secrets.

## Claude · 2026-08-17T20:44:44.613Z

**Use Stripe. Restrict sales to the US. Sign up with your SSN.**

Why:

- **Lemon Squeezy, Paddle and Polar all need an unexpired passport.** Yours is expired, so they're closed today.
- **Stripe doesn't** — SSN and address is enough for a US-based individual.
- **US-only avoids the tax problem.** Selling to the EU/UK/India/Korea creates a VAT obligation from the very first sale. Staying US-only means none of that, and US state tax doesn't bite until ~$100k in one state.
- **IAP isn't worth it for you.** On your $75–100 annual pricing, web checkout keeps ~8.5% versus Apple's 15%. And your Mac app — the main product — can't use IAP at all.

Later, when your passport is renewed and you have real revenue, move to a merchant of record and open up internationally. Switching with 50 customers is easy; with 5,000 it isn't.

**Next:** sign up, make the four products, restrict to US, point the webhook at `https://trysleepwell.app/api/stripe/webhook`, send me the two secrets. Everything else is already built.

# IAP (15% SBP) vs. web checkout via MoR — research notes, 17 Aug 2026

**Source flags:** 🟡 = vendor with skin in the game · 🟢 = independent/primary (court, Apple docs, SEC/earnings, government, research firm)

---

## TOPIC B FIRST — the legal ground moved, and it's moving again this month

This is the most important finding, because it changes the whole calculation.

### Current US state: 0% on external link-outs — still in force today, but actively being litigated *right now*

| Date | Event | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 30 Apr 2025 | Judge Gonzalez Rogers finds Apple in willful civil contempt; bars **any** commission on US external link-outs, bars scare screens | 🟢 [Daring Fireball](https://daringfireball.net/2025/04/gonzales_rogers_apple_app_store_ruling) |
| 1 May 2025 | Apple updates App Review Guidelines 3.1.1, 3.1.1(a), 3.1.3, 3.1.3(a) for US storefront | 🟢 [9to5Mac](https://9to5mac.com/2025/05/01/apple-app-store-guidelines-external-links/) |
| **11 Dec 2025** | **Ninth Circuit affirms contempt but *reverses* the permanent 0% remedy.** Apple may charge fees limited to "costs genuinely and reasonably necessary for its coordination of external links… but no more," plus limited IP compensation; **excludes** security/privacy costs. Court also lets Apple stop devs making external links *more prominent* than IAP. | 🟢 [9th Cir. opinion PDF](https://cdn.ca9.uscourts.gov/datastore/opinions/2025/12/11/25-2935.pdf) · [MacRumors](https://www.macrumors.com/2025/12/11/apple-app-store-fees-external-payment-links/) · [Shinder Cantor Lerner](https://scl-llp.com/ninth-circuit-upholds-apple-contempt-finding-but-narrows-scope-of-remedial-relief/) |
| 4–6 May 2026 | SCOTUS (Kagan) **denies** Apple's emergency stay of the mandate | 🟢 [9to5Mac](https://9to5mac.com/2026/05/06/supreme-court-rejects-apples-stay-request-epic-games-case-to-head-back-to-district-court/) · [Apple's application](https://www.supremecourt.gov/DocketPDF/25/25A1213/407958/20260504154515930_2026-05-04%20Apple-Epic%20SCT%20Application%20to%20Stay%20Mandate.pdf) |
| 30 Jun 2026 | **SCOTUS grants cert** on the contempt finding | 🟢 [IPWatchdog](https://ipwatchdog.com/2026/06/30/high-court-grants-cert-in-apples-challenge-to-ninth-circuit-contempt-ruling-in-app-store-dispute/) |
| 11 Aug 2026 | District court **denies** Apple's bid to pause fee proceedings pending SCOTUS | 🟢 [MacRumors](https://www.macrumors.com/2026/08/11/app-store-epic-games-fee-fight-proceeds/) |
| 12–13 Aug 2026 | SCOTUS grants a 24-hour stay, then rejects further delay | 🟢 [MacRumors](https://www.macrumors.com/2026/08/12/app-store-fee-fight-24-hour-delay/) |
| **13–14 Aug 2026** | **Apple files its proposed link-out commission with the district court** | 🟢 [TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2026/08/14/apple-proposes-to-take-a-15-cut-of-purchases-made-outside-the-app-store/) · [9to5Mac](https://9to5mac.com/2026/08/13/apple-proposes-commissions-of-up-to-15-for-off-app-store-purchases-in-the-us/) |

### Apple's proposed US link-out rates (filed 3 days ago — NOT yet in effect)

- **15%** standard apps
- **10%** subscription renewals + Video/News/Mini Apps Partner Programs
- **5%** Small Business Program apps

Apple benchmarks these to Google Play's 20/15/10. Epic's response: under the Ninth Circuit's "necessary costs" definition the number should be **0%**, and Apple's proposal is "far outside the bounds" of the court's guidance. Michael Tsai's roundup notes Apple's own expert effectively conceded Stripe/Paddle provide full turnkey processing for less than Apple wants for "IP" alone ([mjtsai, 17 Aug 2026](https://mjtsai.com/blog/2026/08/17/apple-proposes-15-external-purchase-fee/)).

**Procedural timeline:** Epic gets ~60 days to file legal analysis, then a district-court hearing. Apple's SCOTUS merits brief is due **14 Sep 2026**; a merits ruling is realistically 2027. Match Group's CFO confirmed on the **11 Aug 2026** earnings call that "zero percent commissions on alternative payments" continue, with resolution expected "later this year, early next year" (🟢 [Motley Fool transcript](https://www.fool.com/earnings/call-transcripts/2026/08/11/match-group-mtch-q2-2026-earnings-call-transcript/)).

&gt; **Planning implication:** a solo dev building on US link-out economics today is building on a rate that Apple is at this moment asking a court to raise to 5% (SBP tier). Apple has already landed on 15% as its global anchor everywhere it has been forced to allow steering — Japan and Brazil both settled at 15% (10% for program participants). Assume 5% is the realistic floor for SBP link-outs by 2027.

### Entitlement / implementation requirements (US)

Apple's own current guidelines, verbatim: *"These entitlements are **not required** for developers to include buttons, external links, or other calls to action in their United States storefront apps."* Same carve-out for reader apps under 3.1.3(a). 🟢 [App Review Guidelines](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/review/guidelines/) · [Apple Developer News](https://developer.apple.com/news/?id=9txfddzf)

⚠️ **Several 2026 third-party "implementation guides" are wrong on this.** [Stora's May 2026 guide](https://stora.sh/blog/2026-05-16-apple-app-store-external-purchase-links-implementation-guide) says the US requires the `external-purchase-link` entitlement, a system disclosure sheet, and 27%/12% commission. That describes the *pre-injunction* global entitlement, not the US today. Trust Apple's guidelines page, not the guides.

What **is** still required in the US:
- **You must still offer IAP alongside** the link (Guideline 3.1.1). Cal AI ($50M ARR) was **pulled from the App Store in April 2026** for embedding a Stripe flow *instead of* IAP, plus deceptive billing design (3.1.2c) and manipulative tactics (Code of Conduct 5.6). Reinstated after ~4 days. 🟢 [TechCrunch, 21 Apr 2026](https://techcrunch.com/2026/04/21/apples-cal-ai-crackdown-signals-its-still-policing-the-app-store/) · [MacRumors](https://www.macrumors.com/2026/04/21/apple-cal-ai-app-store-removal/)
- **Link must open in the system browser**, not an in-app browser. Apple forced Patreon to move its checkout out of an in-app webview within ~10 days of launch. 🟢 [TechCrunch, 16 May 2025](https://techcrunch.com/2025/05/16/after-adding-its-own-billing-option-on-ios-apple-asks-patreon-to-move-it-to-an-external-browser)
- **US-storefront gating.** The carve-out is US-only; the same UI elsewhere is a violation.
- Post-Dec 2025, Apple may restrict the external link from being *more prominent* than the IAP button (font/size/placement), while having to permit parity.

### Small Business Program — unchanged in 2026 🟢 [Apple](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/small-business-program/)

15% on paid apps and IAP; threshold **$1M USD in proceeds** (net of Apple's commission and certain taxes) in the prior calendar year; new developers qualify automatically; must list Associated Developer Accounts; **annual re-enrollment required**; exceeding $1M mid-year moves you to standard rate for future sales; re-qualify the year after dropping back below. Rate change takes effect 15 days after the fiscal month in which enrollment is approved. No 2026 changes found in Apple's developer news.

Note: the threshold counts **App Store proceeds only** — web revenue does not push you over $1M.

### EU (DMA) as of 2026 🟢/🟡 [RevenueCat](https://www.revenuecat.com/blog/growth/apple-eu-dma-update-june-2025/) · [Funnelfox, 10 Apr 2026](https://blog.funnelfox.com/apple-app-store-fees-2026-eu-dma/) · [MacRumors](https://www.macrumors.com/2025/06/26/app-store-eu-rule-change-dma/)

The €0.50/install Core Technology Fee **was retired 1 Jan 2026**, replaced by a 5% **Core Technology Commission**. Current stack for external-link sales:

| Component | Standard | Small Business Program |
|---|---|---|
| Initial acquisition (new users, first 6mo) | 2% | 0% |
| Store Services Tier 1 (basic listing) | 5% | 5% |
| Store Services Tier 2 (search, featuring, reviews, analytics) | 13% | 10% |
| Core Technology Commission | 5% | 5% |
| **Effective total, SBP + Tier 2** | ~20% | **~15–17%** |
| **Effective total, SBP + Tier 1 only** | ~12% | **~10%** |

Plus your own payment processing. **The EU link-out is not free** — dropping to Tier 1 (giving up App Store search placement, featuring, and reviews) is the only way to get meaningfully below IAP.

### Japan / Brazil
- **Japan (MSCA), effective 18 Dec 2025:** 15% on purchases within 7 days of tapping a link (10% for program participants). Unlike the US, Japan **requires** side-by-side presentation of Apple IAP. Terms acceptance deadline was 17 Mar 2026. 🟢 [9to5Mac](https://9to5mac.com/2025/12/17/apple-announces-sweeping-app-store-and-iphone-changes-in-japan/) · [MacRumors](https://www.macrumors.com/2025/12/17/japan-app-store-feature-updates/)
- **Brazil (CADE settlement, approved Dec 2025):** 15% steering commission. 🟢 [9to5Mac](https://9to5mac.com/2025/12/23/apple-settles-brazilian-antitrust-case-with-app-store-policy-overhaul/)
