## TOPIC A — the hybrid question

### The single best experiment: RevenueCat / Dipsea 🟡 (RevenueCat owns Dipsea and sells web billing)

**Design:** started 7 May 2025, ran 3+ weeks, **~12,500 users, ~3,100 per variant**. Variant C offered Apple IAP at standard price *with a 7-day free trial* vs. a web annual subscription at **30% off**. [Full writeup, 14 May 2025](https://www.revenuecat.com/blog/growth/iap-vs-web-purchases-conversion-test/)

| Variant | Initial conv. | Trial start | Trial→paid | Paid conv. | Rev/customer @30% | Rev/customer @**15% SBP** |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| B — IAP only | **27.0%** | 25.6% | 25.0% | 6.3% | $2.09 | **$2.53** |
| C — IAP + discounted web | 23.5% | 22.0% | **28.2%** | 5.9% | $1.94 | $2.17 |
| D — Web only | 18.1% | 15.9% | 26.3% | 5.3% | $1.96 | $1.96 |

**Share choosing web: ~36% of Variant C revenue came via web** — and that was *with a 30% discount dangled*. No public data exists on the split at price parity.

**The decisive line for your situation:** at Small Business Program rates, IAP-only produced **$2.53 per customer vs $2.17 for the hybrid and $1.96 for web-only**. Adding web checkout *destroyed* ~14% of revenue per customer at 15%. Gruber's [15 May 2025 take](https://daringfireball.net/linked/2025/05/15/revenuecat-external-purchase-report): "IAP really is more convenient."

**Longer-run follow-up, [14 Apr 2026](https://www.revenuecat.com/blog/growth/why-you-shouldnt-use-app-to-web) — RevenueCat's own article is titled *"Why you shouldn't use app-to-web checkout"*:**

| Metric | IAP only | IAP + Web | Δ |
|---|---|---|---|
| Conversion to paying | 7.8% | 7.4% | −5.1% |
| Active subscribers | 211 | 209 | −1% |
| **Set to renew** | 46 | **101** | **+119%** |
| Churned | 121 | 104 | −15% |
| **Refunded customers** | 28 | **38** | **+36%** |

Note the honest caveat RevenueCat itself makes: the renewal advantage is attributed to users *forgetting to cancel* on web, not to satisfaction. Related datapoint (n=2,000 May subscriptions): **2.5% of web subscribers had auto-renew off vs 18% of App Store subscribers.** That's a real LTV effect — and also a refund/chargeback and reputational liability.

### Superwall 🟡 — [7 May 2025](https://superwall.com/blog/initial-data-is-in-app-to-web-conversion-rates-after-the-app-store-ruling), no sample size disclosed
- Initial conversion: **−11.9%**
- Net proceeds at 30% commission: **+19.85%**
- **At 15% Small Business Program: net proceeds *fall* by ~1.30%.** Only if trial conversion also rises ~20% does it become +17.74%.

No 2026 follow-up published despite promising one — flagging that gap.

### Paddle aggregate 🟡 — [external payments guide, 2026](https://www.paddle.com/resources/external-payments-guide-ios-app-developers), "aggregated from Paddle customers &amp; RevenueCat content," **no methodology, no sample sizes**
- Net proceeds: +24% (good) / +15% (average, SBP baseline)
- Conversion change: 0 to +1% (good) / **−5% to −12% (average)**
- LTV: +17% (good) / +10% (average)
- Named cases: Stoikk +25% est. revenue; Runna (~1M MAU) web subscribers "15% higher" retention; HubX "$100,000+ in 72 days"; Nexus Mods "9x revenue uplift in China"
- RevenueCat aggregate cited: initial conversion dips of "10–15%"

### What real companies actually disclosed post-30 Apr 2025

| Company | What they said | Numbers? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Match Group** (Tinder/Hinge) | Rolled out alternative payments across portfolio Q4 2025 | **~$14M savings Q4 2025**, **~$90M expected 2026** → **raised to $130M** ("$20M better than expected") | 🟢 [PR Newswire, 4 Nov 2025](https://www.prnewswire.com/news-releases/match-group-announces-third-quarter-results-302604631.html) · [Q2 2026 call, 11 Aug 2026](https://www.fool.com/earnings/call-transcripts/2026/08/11/match-group-mtch-q2-2026-earnings-call-transcript/) |
| **Bumble** | CFO Kevin Cook: "direct payments contributed **approximately one percentage point** of YoY gross margin expansion" (Q4 25); "**more than half of our U.S. iOS payments** being made through Apple Pay" (early Q1 26); "**We have not seen any friction**… there seems to be an **improvement in renewals**… somewhat unexpected" | Q1 26 GM **+300bps**; Q2 26 GM **+380bps** YoY, cost of revenue 26% vs 29% | 🟢 [Q4 25 call, 11 Mar 2026](https://www.fool.com/earnings/call-transcripts/2026/03/11/bumble-bmbl-q4-2025-earnings-call-transcript/) · Q1/Q2 2026 calls |
| **Spotify** | Amicus brief: "internal data show… a **significant increase** in iOS users upgrading to a Premium subscription"; iOS conversion up "substantially" over 2 weeks while Android stayed flat | **Declined to disclose any numbers** | 🟢 [TechCrunch, 21 May 2025](https://techcrunch.com/2025/05/21/spotify-says-support-for-external-payments-on-ios-has-already-boosted-subscriptions/) |
| **Epic** | Boosted Epic Rewards **5% → 20%** cashback for its own payment system, permanent for Fortnite/Rocket League/Fall Guys; **0% rewards** on Apple/Google IAP | Bribe size disclosed; results not | 🟢 [Epic Games Store](https://store.epicgames.com/en-US/news/better-deals-in-fortnite-and-more-players-get-20-back-in-epic-rewards-starting-today) |
| **Patreon** | Shipped web payments 6 May 2025 (card, Venmo, PayPal, Apple Pay); IAP relegated to small text under the bold "Join" button; forced by Apple to move out of in-app browser | ~95% of active creators on subscription billing; no conversion data | 🟢 [TechCrunch](https://techcrunch.com/2025/05/06/patreons-app-can-now-accept-web-payments-after-u-s-app-store-changes/) |
| **Amazon/Kindle** | Added external buy links; approved by Apple | **No public numbers** | 🟢 [MacRumors](https://www.macrumors.com/2025/05/09/fortnite-submitted-to-us-app-store/) |
| **Duolingo** | Analysts noted app store fees = 17% of cost of revenue; testing off-app payments | **No public disclosure found** despite searching Q1/Q2 2026 earnings | [Benzinga, 2 Jul 2025](https://www.benzinga.com/analyst-stock-ratings/analyst-color/25/07/46230517/app-store-fee-cuts-set-to-boost-duolingo-match-bumble-earnings) |
| **Delivery apps** | **Not applicable** — physical goods and real-world services were never subject to IAP anywhere | — | 🟢 Apple Guidelines 3.1.3 / 3.1.5 |

Analyst sensitivity 🟡 (Benzinga/Bloomberg Intelligence, 2 Jul 2025): every 500bp shift of subscription revenue to off-app payments → Duolingo EBITDA +3.3%, Match +2.8%, Bumble +3.0%.

### Ecosystem-level adoption

**⚠️ Honest gap: no public Appfigures, Sensor Tower, or press count exists of how many apps use external purchase links.** I searched all three plus TechCrunch/9to5Mac/MacRumors/Daring Fireball. Sensor Tower's [State of Mobile 2026](https://sensortower.com/blog/state-of-mobile-2026) doesn't mention external payments at all.

Best available proxies:
- **RevenueCat SOSA 2026** 🟡 (115,000 apps, $16B revenue, &gt;1B transactions, 2025 data): **web = 3.2% of subscription revenue globally, 4.9% in North America.** By app tier: **41% of top-decile (Tier 5) apps have web revenue vs 1.3% of Tier 1 hobby apps** — a 31× gap. [Report](https://www.revenuecat.com/state-of-subscription-apps) · [SaaStr summary](https://www.saastr.com/the-top-10-learnings-from-revenuecats-state-of-subscription-apps-how-115000-mobile-apps-deliver-16b-in-revenue-whats-working-whats-quietly-killing-growth/)
- **US App Store consumer spend grew 18.1% in 2025** to $55.5B; global $155.8B, +21.6% 🟢 [Appfigures via TechCrunch, 14 Jan 2026](https://techcrunch.com/2026/01/14/app-downloads-declined-again-in-2025-but-consumer-spending-soared-to-nearly-156b/). **No visible ecosystem-level cannibalization** — link-out adoption is small in aggregate.
- **Appfigures' own verdict**, 2 May 2025 (closest to an independent analyst voice): *"the economics don't support widespread adoption of external payments"* and *"bypassing Apple sounds good, but for most, it's a trap."* [Link](https://appfigures.com/resources/insights/20250502?f=5)

### Incremental or cannibalizing?

**Cannibalizing, on the only controlled evidence available.** The Dipsea test is the only true A/B with a sample size, and the hybrid variant produced *fewer* paying customers and *less* revenue per customer than IAP-only at 15%. The public-company wins (Match, Bumble) are real but come from firms paying **30%/20%**, with dedicated payments teams, huge existing user bases, and brands users already trust with a card.

One useful distinction: 🟡 [Airbridge](https://www.airbridge.io/en/blog/does-web-to-app-improve-profitability) argues *web-to-app* (paid acquisition landing on your own checkout, then handing off to the app) is genuinely incremental when it reaches audiences the App Store wouldn't; *app-to-web* link-outs are near-pure substitution — "if your web funnel is running ads to the same audience that would have found you through the App Store, you will lose 25–35% of conversions and net less revenue."

**⚠️ No solo/indie developer has published real numbers.** I searched Reddit, Indie Hackers, and dev blogs. Every quantified data point in this section comes either from a vendor selling web billing or from a public company at 100–1000× a solo dev's scale.

---

## TOPIC C — non-financial trade-offs

### Customer email: Apple gives you nothing

Developers receive a **transaction ID only** — a 15-digit value with no correlation to any customer identifier. No name, no email, no address. 🟢 [Apple Developer Forums](https://developer.apple.com/forums/thread/111171). What *is* available: aggregate/anonymized Sales &amp; Trends and App Analytics in App Store Connect; `appAccountToken` (a UUID **you** generate and attach to a transaction, letting you link a purchase to *your own* account system — but only if the user created an account with you first); App Store Server API/Notifications for subscription state; reviewer nicknames on reviews. Nothing changed in 2025–26.

Under a merchant of record you get the email on every sale, plus the ability to email, win back, cross-sell, and migrate platforms.

**Quantifying the value — weakly.** The widely cited figure is **$36 return per $1 spent on email marketing** (DMA/Litmus lineage, [Litmus puts it at $36–50](https://www.litmus.com/blog/the-email-metrics-marketers-measure-and-the-ones-they-should)). 🟡 **Treat this as marketing folklore** — it's self-reported survey data with no controlled counterfactual, recycled for a decade. I found **no credible study isolating the value of an owned email list to a solo app founder**. The honest argument is qualitative: it's the only distribution asset you own and the only thing that survives a platform ban, an algorithm change, or a pivot to another platform.

### Refunds

**Under IAP: Apple decides, unilaterally, and you cannot issue or block a refund.** When a customer requests one, Apple sends a `CONSUMPTION_REQUEST` App Store Server Notification and you have **12 hours** to reply via the Send Consumption Information endpoint with usage evidence. Apple's Refund Decisioning System weighs that against the customer's purchase/refund history. **If you don't respond in time, Apple frequently grants the refund by default.** 🟢 [Apple WWDC25 "Dive into App Store server APIs"](https://developer.apple.com/videos/play/wwdc2025/249/) · 🟡 [RevenueCat](https://www.revenuecat.com/blog/company/handle-apple-refund-requests-automatically)

Refund-rate data (thin): Dipsea's App Store refund rate was **3%, dropping to 1.9% over 15 days** once consumption responses were automated 🟡 (RevenueCat, n = one app). RevenueCat's own hybrid test showed **+36% more refunded customers** on the web arm.

**Under an MoR:** Lemon Squeezy/Paddle are the legal seller — they own refunds, chargebacks, and fraud liability, and absorb chargeback fees. You set the policy; they execute and eat the disputes. 🟡 [Paddle vs Lemon Squeezy 2026](https://contracollective.com/blog/paddle-vs-lemon-squeezy-merchant-of-record-digital-commerce-2026)

**⚠️ No public head-to-head refund or chargeback rate comparison for the same app across IAP and web exists** beyond RevenueCat's single test.

### Payout timing

| | Timing | Threshold |
|---|---|---|
| **Apple** | ~**33 days after fiscal month end**, contractually within **45 days**. 12 payments/year on Apple's 4-4-5 fiscal calendar (one 35-day and two 28-day months per quarter) | **$40 USD** minimum (varies by currency/region); shortfalls roll forward |
| **Stripe (direct)** | **T+2** business days rolling for established US accounts; **7–14 day hold** on new accounts | Effectively none |
| **Lemon Squeezy / Paddle** | Scheduled payouts (typically weekly/biweekly), slower than raw Stripe because MoR settlement and tax remittance sit in between | Platform-specific |

🟢 [Apple minimum payment threshold](https://developer.apple.com/help/app-store-connect/reference/reporting/minimum-payment-threshold) · 🟡 [RevenueCat fiscal calendar 2026](https://www.revenuecat.com/blog/growth/apple-fiscal-calendar-year-payment-dates) · 🟢 [Stripe docs](https://docs.stripe.com/connect/manage-payout-schedule)

**Net: web is ~40 days faster to cash.** For a solo dev with no runway, that's a real, non-trivial advantage.

### Subscription mechanics

- **Management UI:** IAP gets Apple's system-level subscription settings, Apple's cancellation flow, Apple's billing-issue prompts, and Apple's win-back/offer-code machinery for free. Web means you build and support the portal, the cancel flow, the dunning emails, and the card-updater — or pay for them.
- **Family Sharing:** Available for auto-renewable subscriptions and non-consumables — one purchase shared with up to **5 family members**, at no extra revenue to you. **Disabled by default** on every IAP; you enable it per-product and the user must opt in. It's a conversion/retention lever with a revenue cost, and it has **no web equivalent** unless you build seats. 🟢 [Apple](https://developer.apple.com/app-store/subscriptions/)
- **Involuntary churn — this is where IAP is strongest.** RevenueCat SOSA 2026 🟡 (115k apps): **~14% of iOS subscription cancellations are involuntary billing failures, vs nearly a third (~33%) on Google Play.** Apple's card-on-file quality, automatic billing retry, and grace periods do heavy lifting you'd otherwise pay for.
- Web comparison 🟡: Recurly network data (Jul 2026) puts monthly involuntary churn at **1.30% at the $10–25/mo tier**, falling to 0.18% above $250 ARPC; Recurly estimates **$129B** in revenue lost to failed subscription payments across 2025. Churnkey claims **up to 70%** of detected involuntary churn is recoverable — smart retries ~40%, card updater ~25%, dunning email/SMS +15–20%; industry median recovery ~47.6%. [Recurly benchmarks](https://recurly.com/research/churn-rate-benchmarks/) · [dunningcompare 2026](https://www.dunningcompare.com/stats/involuntary-churn-statistics-2026). **All vendor-sourced and self-selected** — these are customers of dunning products.

### Trust / friction

**⚠️ I found no study measuring whether buyers trust Apple billing more than an unknown web checkout.** That claim is intuitive but, as far as I can find, unmeasured. What exists as proxy:

- **Baymard Institute** 🟢 (research firm, meta-analysis of **50 studies**): average cart abandonment **70.22%**; **mobile 80.45% vs desktop 68.62%**. Top fixable causes: unexpected extra costs **39%**, forced account creation **19%**, checkout too long/complicated **18%**. [Source](https://baymard.com/lists/cart-abandonment-rate)
- The app-to-web flow triggers all three of those: it adds a tax line the IAP price didn't show, it usually forces account creation, and it's a longer flow — on mobile, the worst-converting surface.
- The measured conversion penalty (Dipsea −13% initial conversion for the hybrid; Superwall −11.9%) is the closest thing to a trust/friction number that exists.
- Mitigation with evidence behind it: **prominently offer Apple Pay on web.** Bumble reports **more than half of its US iOS payments now run through Apple Pay** on its own checkout, with "no friction" observed 🟢.

### VAT / sales tax — what an MoR actually removes

**Without an MoR (plain Stripe), a US solo dev selling worldwide faces:**
- **EU:** **No registration threshold whatsoever** for non-EU sellers of B2C digital services. VAT is due **from the very first sale**, at the customer's local rate (17–27%). The mitigation is the **non-Union OSS** scheme: register in one member state, file one quarterly return covering all 27. 🟡/🟢 [EAS 2026 cheat sheet](https://help.easproject.com/hc/en-gb/articles/5371372277791-EU-VAT-Registration-for-Non-EU-Sellers-2026-B2C-Compliance-Cheat-Sheet) · [Stripe VAT OSS guide](https://stripe.com/guides/introduction-to-eu-vat-and-european-vat-oss) · [VATupdate, 5 Jul 2026](https://www.vatupdate.com/2026/07/05/eu-digital-sales-vat-compliance-and-oss-rules/)
- **UK:** **Nil threshold** for non-established sellers; 20% standard rate. Same "from first sale" logic.
- **US state sales tax on SaaS/digital goods:** economic nexus post-*Wayfair* (commonly $100k or 200 transactions, though many states have dropped the transaction count). A solo dev is unlikely to hit nexus in most states early, but a successful app will in the large ones.
- Plus Australian GST, Canadian GST/HST simplified registration, Japan, Norway, Switzerland, India, etc. — several with low or zero foreign-seller thresholds.

**With Apple:** Apple acts as commissionaire/agent depending on territory and **collects and remits VAT/GST for you in most of the world**. You handle only your own income tax. This is a genuinely large, invisible service.

**With Lemon Squeezy / Paddle:** they become the legal seller and take on the identical burden — EU VAT in every member state, UK VAT, US state sales tax, Australian and Canadian GST, plus chargeback liability. 🟡 [Paddle](https://www.paddle.com/) · [Lemon Squeezy pricing](https://www.lemonsqueezy.com/pricing)

**⚠️ I found no credible estimate of the annual cost or hours of DIY VAT compliance for a small digital seller, and no documented enforcement action against a small foreign seller.** Every source quantifying the pain sells the solution.

### Vendor status check — verify before committing

**Lemon Squeezy's 5% + $0.50 is still the published rate** ([lemonsqueezy.com/pricing](https://www.lemonsqueezy.com/pricing)) and it still operates as an MoR post-Stripe-acquisition. But as of ~April 2026 it **coexists with Stripe Managed Payments (public preview)**, and multiple secondary sources report **onboarding now takes weeks rather than days**. 🟡 [Fungies](https://fungies.io/lemon-squeezy-stripe-acquisition-saas-founders-2026/) · [stilllater](https://stilllater.com/dev-tools/lemonsqueezy-vs-stripe-vs-paddle/) — I could not verify onboarding status from a primary source. **Confirm directly with Lemon Squeezy before building on it.**

**Don't forget the tooling layer:** RevenueCat is free to $2,500 monthly tracked revenue, then **1% of tracked revenue** (MTR is gross, counted *before* Apple's cut). That 1% applies on both paths.

---

## The bottom line for a solo dev at 15%

Stacking real costs on the web path today: **~5% + $0.50 (Lemon Squeezy) + ~1% (RevenueCat) ≈ 7–9% all-in** on a low-priced subscription — versus 15% IAP. That headline gap is why the web looks attractive.

But three things eat it:
1. **Conversion.** The only controlled test shows the hybrid *underperforms* IAP-only at 15% ($2.17 vs $2.53 per customer). Superwall independently models −1.30% net proceeds for an SBP developer.
2. **The rate is about to change.** Apple filed for **5% on SBP link-outs three days ago**. If the district court grants anything near it, the web path costs 5% + 5% + 1% ≈ 11–13% against 15% IAP — and the entire margin evaporates.
3. **Everything Apple does for free** — VAT remittance in ~175 territories, refund arbitration, billing retry (14% involuntary churn vs ~33% on Play), Family Sharing, the cancellation UI, and the trust of a card already on file.

The one thing web buys that IAP genuinely cannot: **the customer's email address**, and the ~40-day-faster cash. If those matter more than 2–6 points of net margin that may not even materialize, that's the real argument — not the fee arithmetic.</result>

**Research is complete and delivered** in my previous message, covering all three topics. The headline findings:

**Topic B is the buried lede and it moved three days ago.** Apple filed a proposed US link-out commission on 13–14 Aug 2026: 15% standard, 10% subscription renewals, **5% for Small Business Program apps**. The 0% rate is still in force today, but the Ninth Circuit (11 Dec 2025) already reversed the permanent 0% remedy, SCOTUS granted cert 30 Jun 2026, and the district court is now setting the number. Apple has settled at 15% everywhere it has been forced to allow steering (Japan Dec 2025, Brazil Dec 2025). Small Business Program terms themselves are unchanged: 15%, $1M proceeds threshold, annual re-enrollment.

**Topic A's controlled evidence points the opposite way from the vendor marketing.** RevenueCat's own Dipsea A/B (~12,500 users, ~3,100/variant) found that at **15% SBP rates, IAP-only earned $2.53 per customer vs $2.17 for the hybrid and $1.96 for web-only** — adding web checkout destroyed ~14% of revenue per customer. Superwall independently models −1.30% net proceeds for an SBP developer. RevenueCat published a follow-up in April 2026 literally titled *"Why you shouldn't use app-to-web checkout."* The big public wins (Match $130M in 2026, Bumble +380bps gross margin) all come from companies paying 20–30%, not 15%.

**Three honest gaps I could not close:** no public count from Appfigures/Sensor Tower/press of how many apps use external links; no solo or indie developer has published real numbers; and no study exists measuring whether buyers trust Apple billing more than an unknown web checkout — that claim is intuitive but unmeasured.

One caveat worth flagging to the user: several 2026 "implementation guides" circulating online are **wrong** about US requirements (claiming the entitlement and a 27% commission are required). Apple's own App Review Guidelines are the authority, and they say no entitlement is needed for the US storefront.

Also note the MCP servers listed as unauthorized (Gmail, Google Drive, Vercel, etc.) played no role here — all research was via web search and fetch.</result>

## What exists now

**Four products, created and priced:**

| Product | Price | Lookup key |
|---|---|---|
| Mac | $75/yr | `mac` |
| All Devices | $100/yr | `all` |
| Family | $199/yr | `family` |
| Family Lifetime | $399 once | `family_lifetime` |

**Webhook** `we_1U5Y20` → `https://trysleepwell.app/api/stripe/webhook`, five events, signing secret generated.

**All three keys in Vercel** across production, preview and development.

**`/api/checkout`** — creates Checkout Sessions. I tested a real one against the Mac price: **$7500 USD, status open, working payment URL.** The plumbing works.

## Two design choices worth knowing

**Prices resolve by `lookup_key`, not hardcoded IDs.** You can change a price in the dashboard without a deploy, and an archived price fails loudly instead of quietly charging the old amount.

**Checkout is restricted to US buyers**, via a country allow-list on address collection — Stripe has no direct switch for digital goods. That's what keeps you out of EU/UK VAT, which starts at the *first* sale. `ALLOWED_COUNTRIES` is the one line to change when you eventually move to a merchant of record.

## What's left

**Yours:** complete Stripe onboarding (`details_submitted` is false — bank details and identity, needed before live mode, not before testing).

**Mine, when you want it:** a `/thanks` page, and wiring the pricing buttons to `/api/checkout`. That last one lives in `web/app/page.tsx`, which the other session owns — so I'd coordinate rather than edit underneath them.

**You can test the whole flow right now** with card `4242 4242 4242 4242`, any future expiry. A completed test purchase should land a row in Supabase.

## Stripe integration task

### Objective
Implement this Stripe integration using the blueprint below. The blueprint describes the required API operations, parameters, and sequencing. Adapt the implementation to your architecture, data models, and tech stack.

### How to use the resources
- Treat the blueprint as the source of truth for required API operations and parameters.
- Prefer reusing outputs from earlier steps as inputs to later ones (e.g., use the created customer ID when creating a subscription).
- Persist and retrieve required Stripe resource identifiers in your datastore (e.g., customer_id, subscription_id) and associate them with existing domain models.
- Do NOT use chapter numbers as names in your code. Name all functions, routes, CLI commands, variables, and files after the domain action they perform (e.g., `create_account`, `/create-checkout-session`, `onboard`) rather than the step they came from (e.g., avoid `chapter1`, `step2`, `/chapter1/create-account`).
- Template variables like ${node...} represent outputs from earlier steps that should be used as inputs to later ones. For example, ${node.createProduct.createProductRequest:default_price} refers to the default_price field from the response of the createProductRequest fixture within the createProduct node.
- If connected to the Stripe MCP server, use tool calls as much as possible to create dependent resources (such as products and prices) if they don't exist already.
- Do not guess as to what the required Stripe API version should be. Leave the API version argument empty when initializing the Stripe client unless the blueprint specifies otherwise.
- When populating .env files or constructing commands for the CLI, use environment variable placeholders for API keys (STRIPE_SECRET_KEY, STRIPE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY) and instruct the user to obtain them from the Stripe Dashboard.

---

### Resources

#### Blueprints (authoritative "what to call")
```toon
title: Set up Managed Payments
steps[4]:
  - key: create-product-chapter
    title: Create a product
    description: Create a digital product with an eligible tax code. View eligibility requirements
    nodes[1]:
      - type: apiRequests
        key: create-subscription-product
        title: API request
        requests[1]:
          - key: create-subscription-product-request
            path: /v1/products
            method: post
            params:
              name: Basic subscription
              description: A basic subscription to our service
              tax_code: txcd_10103100
              default_price_data:
                unit_amount: 1000
                currency: usd
                recurring:
                  interval: month
            requestOptions:
              headers:
                "stripe-version": 2026-02-25.preview
  - key: create-checkout-session-chapter
    title: Create a Checkout Session
    nodes[1]:
      - type: apiRequests
        key: create-checkout-session
        title: API request
        description: "Set the managed_payments[enabled] parameter to true and use 2026.02-25.preview or above as your version header."
        requests[1]:
          - key: create-checkout-session-request
            path: /v1/checkout/sessions
            method: post
            requestOptions:
              headers:
                "stripe-version": 2026-02-25.preview
  - key: complete-checkout-chapter
    title: Test your checkout
    nodes[1]{type,key,title,description,link}:
      uiComponent,complete-checkout,Complete a test payment,"Try different billing addresses to see how tax is calculated for customers in different locations. Then, process a test payment using this test card information.","${node.create-checkout-session-chapter.create-checkout-session.create-checkout-session-request:url}"
  - key: handle-webhooks-chapter
    title: Set up webhooks
    description: Monitor successful payments instantly.
    nodes[1]:
      - type: asyncHandler
        key: wait-for-checkout-completed
        title: Listen for checkout.session.completed
        events[1]{eventType,eventPayloadType}:
          checkout.session.completed,snapshot
        expectedNumberOfEvents: 1
```

## This solved the problem we'd been working around all evening

**You get merchant of record without a passport.** Lemon Squeezy, Paddle and Polar all blocked you on KYC because they're the legal seller. Stripe verifies the account itself — already done.

**And you can sell worldwide again.** The US-only restriction I added an hour ago is gone. Stripe collects and remits VAT, GST and US sales tax everywhere; you register nowhere.

Cost is ~6.4% + 30¢ instead of 2.9% + 30¢. That extra 3.5% buys global tax compliance you genuinely cannot purchase any other way at your size — and it's cheaper than Lemon Squeezy's real all-in rate once its international, PayPal, subscription and payout surcharges are counted.

**One detail worth knowing:** Managed Payments requires an eligible tax code on every product, and rejects the session outright without one. The first attempt failed on exactly that — which is how we discovered the capability was *enabled* rather than missing. All four now carry `txcd_10103100`.
